+
+An unreadable release-control state counts as blocked. A candidate, including a stale or ambiguous candidate, must be resolved by the scheduled reconciler and verified before another release can begin.

+The judgment half remains human work. None of these are detectable by a script, and every one of them has shipped in this repo before:
 
 1. **One theme, or two?** A PR carrying two subjects gets reviewed as neither. If it is two, say so in the description rather than letting the reviewer discover it.
 2. **Is there anything in the diff you would not defend out loud at review?** Scaffolding kept "just in case", a rename half-applied, a workaround with no comment saying why. Cut it now, not after a reviewer asks.
 3. **Did anything land in source whose home is `docs/`?** History, rationale, and status belong in the commit message and `docs/`, per `governance/protocols/code-hygiene.md`.
 4. **Do the commit messages and the PR body describe what actually changed?** Re-read the diff against them. A description written before the last three commits is no longer true.

+---
+name: kael-solve-conflict-maximize
+description: Use at the close of any code, test, or bugfix task, and whenever you are about to write or edit a test, change a gate or its config, add a comment or a header to source, or say a check passed, is fixed, or was already failing. Blocks three shortcuts: weakening or skipping a test or changing a gate to turn it green, leaving comments or watermarks in production code, and reporting verification you did not observe. Canonical: governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md.
+---
+
+# kael-solve-conflict-maximize
+
+Auto-trigger wrapper. The full procedure, the forbidden-move table, and the Claim→Evidence format are canonical in `governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md`. Do not restate them here.
+
+This skill applies at the end of a task, not the beginning. Its job is to stop a green result that did not come from correct code.
+
+## When it fires
+
+- You are about to write or edit a test file, or any file in the protected surface (gates, workflow files, test configs, `settings.json`, hooks).
+- A test is red and the fix you are about to make is in the test or in a gate, not in the code under test.
+- You are adding anything to a file under `apps/`, `packages/`, or `supabase/functions/` that is a comment, a header, a note, or an attribution.
+- You are about to write a final message containing "pass", "done", "fixed", "verified", "pre-existing", or a test count.
+
+## The three rules
+
+1. **Gates are the oracle.** Make the code correct, not the check green. Do not skip, delete, weaken, or snapshot-update a test to pass it. Do not edit a protected gate to pass it. If you must change a protected path, Tu approves it in chat, and it appears on its own line in the final report. After two code changes that leave the same gate red, stop and report the unedited output.
+2. **No comments, no watermarks in production source.** On added lines in production code, write no comment token: no line comment, block comment, JSDoc, authority citation, or trailing comment. Put no AI, author, license, or generated-by marker in any source file. Do not split code across lines to make room for a comment.
+3. **Only observed verification is reported.** Every claim in the final message maps to a command you ran, its exit code, and a count copied from its output. A bug is fixed only after the original repro passes again. "Pre-existing" needs a baseline on a clean tree. Every `file:line` you cite is checked in this turn. Anything not run goes under `Not run:`.
+
+## Commands
+
+```bash
+pnpm lint:test-integrity --working
+pnpm lint:added-hygiene --working
+pnpm lint:comments --working
+```
+
+Exit `0` is clean, `1` means blocking findings, `2` means the checker could not run. A `2` is not a pass; name it.
+
+## Honest limits
+
+These checks catch what is visible in a diff. They do not catch a weak assertion that still runs real code, or a test that checks the wrong property well. Those are reviewer findings, and this skill does not claim to stop them.
+
+## Close
+
+Do not report a task as done until the Claim→Evidence block is filled. Its fields are the only place the result appears:
+
+```text
+Claims -> evidence:
+Not run:
+Pre-existing (baseline):
+Gate or protected-surface changes:
+Comments / watermark (lint result):
+Stop-rule triggers:
+```
+
+An empty `Not run:` must say "none" and why. Do not claim done while a blocking finding is open, while a checker exited `2`, or while a claim has no evidence row.

@@ -0,0 +1,73 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env node
+// Stop hook for kael-solve-conflict-maximize. Canonical: governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md.
+// Blocks a stop (exit 2) only when a checker reports a blocking finding on the uncommitted change.
+// Infrastructure failures allow the stop, so a broken checker cannot block every session.
+
+import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
+import { join } from 'node:path'
+import { changedPathsFromPorcelainV1Z } from '../../scripts/lib/git-status.mjs'
+
+const CHECKERS = ['scripts/check-test-integrity.mjs', 'scripts/check-added-source-hygiene.mjs']
+
+function readStdin() {
+  return new Promise((resolve) => {
+    let data = ''
+    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8')
+    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk) => (data += chunk))
+    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolve(data))
+    const timer = setTimeout(() => resolve(data), 2000)
+    if (typeof timer.unref === 'function') timer.unref()
+  })
+}
+
+function allowStop(note) {
+  if (note) console.error(`[verify-test-integrity] ${note}`)
+  process.exit(0)
+}
+
+await readStdin()
+
+const projectDir = process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd()
+
+let status = ''
+try {
+  status = execFileSync('git', ['status', '--porcelain=v1', '-z'], {
+    cwd: projectDir,
+    encoding: 'utf8',
+    maxBuffer: 1 << 28,
+    stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
+  })
+} catch {
+  allowStop('git unavailable; not blocking')
+}
+if (changedPathsFromPorcelainV1Z(status).length === 0) process.exit(0)
+
+const blockers = []
+for (const checker of CHECKERS) {
+  let output = ''
+  let code = 0
+  try {
+    output = execFileSync(process.execPath, [join(projectDir, checker), '--working'], {
+      cwd: projectDir,
+      encoding: 'utf8',
+      maxBuffer: 1 << 28,
+      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
+    })
+  } catch (error) {
+    code = typeof error.status === 'number' ? error.status : -1
+    output = `${error.stdout || ''}${error.stderr || ''}`
+  }
+  if (code === 1) blockers.push(output.trim())
+  else if (code !== 0) console.error(`[verify-test-integrity] ${checker} could not run (exit ${code}); not blocking on it`)
+}
+
+if (blockers.length === 0) process.exit(0)
+
+console.error(
+  `${blockers.join('\n\n')}\n\n` +
+    'Blocking findings for kael-solve-conflict-maximize. Do not claim done. ' +
+    'Fix the code instead of the test or gate; list any protected-surface change under ' +
+    '"Gate or protected-surface changes"; put comments and watermarks nowhere in production source. ' +
+    'Recheck: pnpm lint:test-integrity --working and pnpm lint:added-hygiene --working',
+)
+process.exit(2)

 # kael-ship
 
-Delivery gate. It runs before a push or a pull request, not after — once a branch is pushed the reviewer's time is already spent, and every workflow in `.github/workflows/` triggers only on `pull_request` or push-to-main, so a branch pushed before its PR exists is checked by nothing.
+Delivery and release gate. `pnpm ship:check` remains the pre-push machine gate. Production release is a separate, non-repeatable local operation with exact-source review, CI, project, candidate, credential, and release-evidence gates.
 
-Two halves. The command owns what a script can prove. You own the rest.
+## Preconditions
 
-## Machine half
+The Production lane is gated. Before calling the runner, confirm all of the following from current evidence:
+
+- Tu's current task explicitly asks for a Production release before `--execute` is used. The skill does not authorize commits, pushes, PR creation, or merges.
+- The local checkout is clean `main`, `HEAD` equals `--source-sha`, and GitHub `main` still points at that SHA.
+- The SHA is the merge commit of one closed PR into `main`, with a current human `APPROVED` review by `kouuuuuuuuu` on the PR's exact head SHA, from someone other than the author.
+- The `release-production.yml` push run and its exact-SHA `quality` job completed successfully.
+- The workflow and the Supabase CLI wrapper both resolve to the registered Production project `iwevizmsedyqozxlawwl`; release control is readable and has no active or unresolved candidate.
+- Required variables are set in the local process: `KAEL_DURABLE_GUARDS_ENABLED=true`, `KAEL_AI_KILL_SWITCH=false`, `NESTSCOUT_ANDROID_FCM_V1_READY=true`, `NESTSCOUT_IOS_APNS_READY=true`, and `NESTSCOUT_PUSH_RECEIPT_RECONCILER_READY=true`.
+- The OS credential store is available. On Windows, use Windows Credential Manager; on Linux, use Secret Service. Do not use `.env`, skill text, command arguments, shell history, or logs for secrets. Set or rotate a value with the masked prompt: `pnpm release:credentials:set -- SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`.
+- The Supabase PAT is scoped to the exact Production project. Grant Edge Functions and Edge Function Secrets read/write access. The current migration flow also runs `supabase link`, so the PAT needs Project Settings, API Keys, and API Key Secrets read access for this same project. Supabase currently limits custom PAT expiry to one year; rotate it before expiry. Do not use a legacy account-wide token or reuse the CI credential. The separate `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD` is needed for the expand-only migration and is not restricted by PAT scopes; protect it separately in the OS credential store.
+- The credential store has these other values by name: `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY`, `VIETMAP_API_KEY`, `EXPO_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `SYSTEM_HEALTH_MONITOR_SECRET`, `SYSTEM_HEALTH_OBSERVER_SECRET`, `PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_EMAIL`, `PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_PASSWORD`, `PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_EMAIL`, and `PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_PASSWORD`.
+- EAS authentication resolves to the registered `@nestscout/home-services` project. Use the pinned `eas-cli@22.0.0`; build identity must match the exact source SHA, app version, platform, and explicit build number in `apps/mobile/config/release-client-policy.json` (`autoIncrement` is off).
+
+The runner checks these conditions again. It reports credential **names** only. It never prints or stores secret values in the repository.
+
+## Degraded lane
+
+Without a secure credential store, GitHub authentication, current review, green exact-SHA CI, the exact project, readable release control, or required release configuration, run only the read-only preflight and report the named missing or blocked gates. Do not start any Production write, EAS build, or store submission. Never turn an unknown check into a pass.
+
+An unreadable release-control state counts as blocked. A candidate, including a stale or ambiguous candidate, must be resolved by the scheduled reconciler and verified before another release can begin.
+
+## Pre-push machine half
 
 ```bash
 pnpm ship:check
 ```
 
-Read **both** of its lists. The gate list is the easy half. The `not run by this command` list is the one that matters: `governance/critical.md` §3 says a gate that could not run is not a gate that passed. Whatever is named there stays unproven locally, and saying "checks pass" without naming it is a false completion report.
+Read **both** lists. The gate list is the easy half. The `not run by this command` list is the one that matters: `governance/critical.md` §3 says a gate that could not run is not a gate that passed. Whatever is named there stays unproven locally, and saying "checks pass" without naming it is a false completion report.
 
 `pnpm lint:residue` is the one gate in that set most likely to be new to you — it catches a focused or skipped test, a leftover debug probe, and a `console.log` in runtime code.
 
-## Judgment half
+## Local Production release
+
+The default command performs read checks and does not write Production:
+
+```bash
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-merge-sha>
+```
+
+It verifies exact clean `main`, current PR approval, exact-SHA CI, the locked Supabase project, secure credentials, scoped-token format, PAT read access, EAS project identity, and the absence of a conflicting candidate. A blocked check stops execution. PAT read access is tested against the exact project; the runner cannot prove the token's exclusive scope or write permissions without performing a write, so create it with only the scopes in the precondition and runbook.
+
+Use `--execute` only when the current task explicitly requests the Production release:
+
+```bash
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-merge-sha> --execute
+```
+
+The local runner is the standard Stage 1 release executor. It builds and verifies the immutable bundle, checks Cloudflare and Supabase baselines, attests exact EAS binaries, verifies expand-only migrations and backend parity evidence, applies the migrations through `pnpm supabase`, configures the synthetic canary, deploys and attests Supabase Edge Functions and the System Health Observer, runs three full synthetic smokes, atomically promotes, and verifies hosted state. The release has a 90-minute overall deadline, the canary is bounded to 80 minutes, and each recovery step to 15 minutes. A candidate therefore reaches stale reconciliation within 110 minutes of canary setup, inside the existing two-hour window. After a configured canary fails, it runs the release-control recovery, cleanup, Edge/Cloudflare restoration, and rollback proof. Report `rollback=UNPROVEN` whenever the proof does not pass; do not retry a partial release blindly.
+
+The standalone step source is `scripts/harness/production-release-steps.yml`. Supabase CLI access must go through `pnpm supabase` and its fixed Production target wrapper, including for isolated migration workdirs.
+
+## Expo submission
+
+The release builds and attests store binaries for the exact source. Submit only a platform named in the current task, only after Production promotion and successful binary attestation:
+
+```bash
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-merge-sha> --submit-platform ios --execute
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-merge-sha> --submit-platform android --execute
+```
+
+Use `--submit-platform all` only when Tu asks for both platforms. Submission uses the attested EAS build ID with `eas-cli@22.0.0`. This flow does not run OTA updates or public store rollout. Report EAS upload separately from App Store / Play processing; report tester installation only when a real device proves it. EAS upload success is not store processing or device evidence.
+
+## Evidence and judgment half
+
+Production machine evidence is written under `artifacts/production-release-<run-id>/`; the redacted operator report is written under `docs/test-logs/`. The report lists the exact source SHA, project, step outcomes, rollback status, and (when requested) separate upload, store-processing, and tester-install states. Never copy a token, signed artifact URL, synthetic password, or raw CLI output into tracked docs.
 
-None of these are detectable by a script, and every one of them has shipped in this repo before:
+The judgment half remains human work. None of these are detectable by a script, and every one of them has shipped in this repo before:
 
 1. **One theme, or two?** A PR carrying two subjects gets reviewed as neither. If it is two, say so in the description rather than letting the reviewer discover it.
 2. **Is there anything in the diff you would not defend out loud at review?** Scaffolding kept "just in case", a rename half-applied, a workaround with no comment saying why. Cut it now, not after a reviewer asks.
 3. **Did anything land in source whose home is `docs/`?** History, rationale, and status belong in the commit message and `docs/`, per `governance/protocols/code-hygiene.md`.
 4. **Do the commit messages and the PR body describe what actually changed?** Re-read the diff against them. A description written before the last three commits is no longer true.
 5. **Was anything called done that a gate could not prove?** Name it in the PR body, not only in your own head.
-6. **Session Memory Gate.** `governance/critical.md` §3 — if this session produced a decision, an executed plan section, an environment change, or an honest gap, the entry is written before the work is reported complete.
+6. **Session Memory Gate.** `governance/critical.md` §3 — if this session produced a decision, an executed plan section, an environment change, or an honest gap, prepare the entry using `docs/memory/INDEX.md` and show it to Tu before writing.

@@ -0,0 +1,52 @@
+---
+name: kael-solve-conflict-maximize
+description: Use at the close of any code, test, or bugfix task, and whenever you are about to write or edit a test, change a gate or its config, add a comment or a header to source, or say a check passed, is fixed, or was already failing. Blocks three shortcuts: weakening or skipping a test or changing a gate to turn it green, leaving comments or watermarks in production code, and reporting verification you did not observe. Canonical: governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md.
+---
+
+# kael-solve-conflict-maximize
+
+Auto-trigger wrapper. The full procedure, the forbidden-move table, and the Claim→Evidence format are canonical in `governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md`. Do not restate them here.
+
+This skill applies at the end of a task, not the beginning. Its job is to stop a green result that did not come from correct code.
+
+## When it fires
+
+- You are about to write or edit a test file, or any file in the protected surface (gates, workflow files, test configs, `settings.json`, hooks).
+- A test is red and the fix you are about to make is in the test or in a gate, not in the code under test.
+- You are adding anything to a file under `apps/`, `packages/`, or `supabase/functions/` that is a comment, a header, a note, or an attribution.
+- You are about to write a final message containing "pass", "done", "fixed", "verified", "pre-existing", or a test count.
+
+## The three rules
+
+1. **Gates are the oracle.** Make the code correct, not the check green. Do not skip, delete, weaken, or snapshot-update a test to pass it. Do not edit a protected gate to pass it. If you must change a protected path, Tu approves it in chat, and it appears on its own line in the final report. After two code changes that leave the same gate red, stop and report the unedited output.
+2. **No comments, no watermarks in production source.** On added lines in production code, write no comment token: no line comment, block comment, JSDoc, authority citation, or trailing comment. Put no AI, author, license, or generated-by marker in any source file. Do not split code across lines to make room for a comment.
+3. **Only observed verification is reported.** Every claim in the final message maps to a command you ran, its exit code, and a count copied from its output. A bug is fixed only after the original repro passes again. "Pre-existing" needs a baseline on a clean tree. Every `file:line` you cite is checked in this turn. Anything not run goes under `Not run:`.
+
+## Commands
+
+```bash
+pnpm lint:test-integrity --working
+pnpm lint:added-hygiene --working
+pnpm lint:comments --working
+```
+
+Exit `0` is clean, `1` means blocking findings, `2` means the checker could not run. A `2` is not a pass; name it.
+
+## Honest limits
+
+These checks catch what is visible in a diff. They do not catch a weak assertion that still runs real code, or a test that checks the wrong property well. Those are reviewer findings, and this skill does not claim to stop them.
+
+## Close
+
+Do not report a task as done until the Claim→Evidence block is filled. Its fields are the only place the result appears:
+
+```text
+Claims -> evidence:
+Not run:
+Pre-existing (baseline):
+Gate or protected-surface changes:
+Comments / watermark (lint result):
+Stop-rule triggers:
+```
+
+An empty `Not run:` must say "none" and why. Do not claim done while a blocking finding is open, while a checker exited `2`, or while a claim has no evidence row.

 - **What burned September was agent behaviour, not only duplication.** One session pushed about 12 pull requests two or three times each, waited on a `CONFLICTING` pull request that never gets CI, and dispatched the Production release lane repeatedly (28 to 58 minutes each). Open pull requests as drafts, check `mergeStateStatus` before waiting, fix locally, mark ready once, and diagnose a failed smoke before dispatching again.

+# Local Production release
+
+`kael-ship` keeps `pnpm ship:check` as the pre-push gate. The standard Stage 1 Production release runs from a clean local `main` checkout through `pnpm release:production`; `release-production.yml` runs main quality checks and the scheduled stale-canary reconciler only. GitHub Actions must not dispatch this standard release.
+
+This runner does not manage the separate Backend System Health or Plan55 Production release workflows. Those specialist lanes retain their existing, separately governed gates.
+
+The runner defaults to a read-only preflight. `--execute` is for a task whose current request explicitly authorizes a Production release. It does not grant commit, push, pull-request, or merge authority.
+
+## Preflight
+
+Run the preflight against the exact merged commit:
+
+```powershell
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-main-merge-sha>
+```
+
+The runner requires a clean local `main` at that SHA, remote `main` at the same SHA, a merged pull request with the current required approval, successful `release-production.yml` quality checks for that exact SHA, the locked Production project ref, no unresolved release candidate, available local credentials and configuration, and the registered Expo project. Any unknown gate is `BLOCKED`; it does not start the release pipeline.
+
+Use a new scoped Supabase personal access token for this machine. In Supabase account settings, create a scoped token limited to the Production project `iwevizmsedyqozxlawwl`. Grant Edge Functions read/write and Edge Function Secrets read/write. Also grant Project Settings, API Keys, and API Key Secrets read access because `supabase link` uses those read APIs. Do not use a legacy account-wide PAT. Supabase currently supports custom PAT expiry up to one year; schedule rotation before its chosen expiry. The CLI accepts a scoped token through `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`; this runner reads it from the OS credential store and places it only in child-process environments. [Supabase PAT guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/personal-access-tokens)
+
+Preflight verifies the scoped-token format and read access to Functions and Function Secrets on the exact project. Supabase does not document a CLI endpoint that exposes a token's complete grant list, so preflight cannot prove the token has no other grants or test its write permissions without a write. Set the project and permissions exactly in the Supabase token creation screen.
+
+`supabase db push` connects with `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`; that database connection is not constrained by the PAT permission scopes. The separate database password is required in the same protected local credential store, and the pipeline still limits migrations to the reviewed expand-only inventory. [Supabase CLI authentication](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/cli/supabase-test)
+
+Store each required credential with the hidden prompt; the name is validated against a fixed allow-list and the value is never an argument:
+
+```powershell
+pnpm release:credentials:set -- SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN
+pnpm release:credentials:set -- SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD
+```
+
+Repeat for the other credential names in `.claude/skills/kael-ship/SKILL.md`. Windows uses Credential Manager; Linux requires Secret Service (`secret-tool`). An unavailable credential store, absent secret, or missing release variable blocks the runner. Do not paste credentials into a command, `.env` file, repository, or report.
+
+## Execute a release
+
+Only after reviewing a passing preflight, and only when the current task explicitly requests a Production release, run:
+
+```powershell
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <40-character-main-merge-sha> --execute
+```
+
+The local pipeline uses pinned EAS CLI `22.0.0`, verifies the registered Expo project, reuses or builds store binaries bound to the exact commit and app version, and attests their build IDs and explicit build numbers before Production writes begin. It then runs the existing Stage 1 release bundle, target checks, migration and backend parity gates, canary, Edge deployments, source attestation, three complete synthetic smokes, and atomic promotion. The full release has a 90-minute deadline. The canary is limited to 80 minutes and each recovery step to 15 minutes, so a configured candidate reaches scheduled stale reconciliation within 110 minutes of setup and the server's two-hour stale-candidate cutoff. If a failure occurs after canary setup, it runs the rollback/reconciliation steps and writes proof; an unproven recovery is reported as `UNPROVEN`.
+
+Expo submission is a separate optional operation. Name the platform in the current task and pass it explicitly:
+
+```powershell
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <sha> --submit-platform ios --execute
+pnpm release:production -- --source-sha <sha> --submit-platform android --execute
+```
+
+`all` submits both platforms only when the task names both. Submission sends only an exact-source build ID already present in the runner's attestation. The report records upload separately from store processing and tester installation; an EAS success does not prove App Store Connect processing, tester availability, or device installation. This flow does not perform OTA updates or public store rollout. [Expo EAS Submit for TestFlight](https://docs.expo.dev/submit/testflight/)
+
+## Evidence and recovery
+
+The machine receipt and step evidence go under the ignored `artifacts/production-release-<run-id>/` directory. A secret-redacted human report goes under `docs/test-logs/`. Reports name the exact source SHA, locked Production project, steps, submission result, and rollback status. Review them before archiving or copying evidence elsewhere.
+
+The scheduled `stale-canary-reconciler` remains the recovery path if the local process or machine stops during a candidate. A new local release preflight blocks while an active or ambiguous candidate exists. Never infer recovery from a process exit alone; use the runner's rollback proof or the next reconciler evidence.

-

-
-## What a verification release proves
-
-- The bound assertions of every catalog entry ran and passed on the merged commit.
-- The build deploys, registers, and serves on Production: three consecutive full synthetic smokes pass on a dedicated cohort, which is then cleaned and proven clean.

-## What it does not prove

+# Protocol: `kael-solve-conflict-maximize`
+
+Canonical procedure for the `kael-solve-conflict-maximize` skill (`.claude/skills/kael-solve-conflict-maximize`, mirrored to `.agents/skills`). The skill body is the trigger; this file is the method.
+
+It exists because coding agents reach "done" by the cheapest path available, and the cheapest path is usually one that makes a check green without making the code correct. Three shapes of that path are blocked here: changing the test or the gate instead of the code, leaving authorship residue in production source, and reporting a verification result that was never observed.
+
+## Evidence this protocol answers to
+
+`docs/test-debt-ledger.md` records what happened without this discipline:
+
+- 233 cases asserted substrings of `.sql` migrations. A `toContain` on a migration passes whether or not the migration ran.
+- 27 cases declared an object literal with a `satisfies` clause, then asserted the same literal back. The runtime half was theatre.
+- 163 cases read file text and asserted nothing a reader could act on, such as `expect(pkg.name).toBe('nestscout')`.
+
+Memory records the reporting side of the same failure: a green count reported as proof, a revert reported as recovery, a "pre-existing" failure claimed without a baseline, and a doc's scope claim repeated after the config contradicted it.
+
+## Scope and reach
+
+- Pillar A (test integrity) applies whenever a diff touches a test file, a gate, or a protected surface.
+- Pillar B (source hygiene) applies to every added line in `apps/`, `packages/`, `supabase/functions/` `.ts`/`.tsx` files.
+- Pillar C (final claims) applies to every final message that reports pass, done, fixed, or pre-existing.
+
+Reach `T` (one known file, no behavior change) still runs Pillar B and Pillar C. Pillar A runs only when a test or gate changed.
+
+## Pillar A: test integrity
+
+### Protected surface
+
+Gates are the oracle. An agent does not get to rewrite the oracle to pass. These paths are protected:
+
+```text
+scripts/check-*.mjs              scripts/harness/**
+scripts/structure-baseline.json  scripts/lint-structure.mjs
+config/harness/manifest.json     **/jest.config.*   **/vitest.config.*
+.github/workflows/**             .claude/settings*.json   .claude/hooks/**
+docs/test-debt-ledger.md
+```
+
+Editing a protected path is allowed when Tu approves it in the current conversation. It must still appear on its own line in the final report, as `Gate or protected-surface changes:`. The checker lists these files and never hides them. It does not block them on its own, because a legitimate gate fix is sometimes the right change.
+
+If the test went red and the fix to the code did not make it green, the answer is not to edit the gate. Count the attempts. After two code changes that leave the same gate red, stop and report the unedited output (Stop rule below).
+
+### Forbidden moves
+
+| Move | Detector | Enforcement |
+|---|---|---|
+| Delete or weaken an assertion so the test passes | `check-test-integrity`: net drop in `expect(`/`assert` count per test file | Blocks |
+| Add `.skip`, `.only`, `.todo`, `xit`, `xdescribe`, `xtest` | `check-test-integrity` on added lines | Blocks |
+| Add a snapshot assertion or run snapshot update (`-u`, `--updateSnapshot`) | `check-test-integrity` on added lines | Blocks |
+| Test file with no assertion at all | `check-test-integrity` on the current file | Blocks |
+| `expect(true)` / `expect(false)` as proof | `check-test-integrity` on added lines | Blocks |
+| Type escapes in a test (`as any`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, `@ts-nocheck`) | `check-test-integrity` on added lines | Blocks |
+| Change an expected literal in the same change as the implementation | Heuristic, shown as a report line | Reports |
+| Swallow an error (`try {} catch {}`) or return early before an assertion | Judgment | Reviewer |
+| Derive the expected value from the implementation instead of a spec, fixture, or hand calculation | Judgment, see oracle rule | Reviewer |
+| Narrow collection (`testRegex`, `include`, `--passWithNoTests`) | Protected surface | Reports |
+
+A net assertion drop is blocked because it is the most common way a red test turns green. A legitimate removal (a duplicate, or an assertion superseded by a stronger one) is still a removal. Put it in the final report under `Gate or protected-surface changes` and ask Tu to clear it. The checker does not accept an excuse.
+
+### Red-first
+
+A regression test must be observed failing on the code before the fix. Record the command and its red output in the Claim→Evidence block. A test that has never been red has not been shown to test anything.
+
+### Oracle rule
+
+Expected values come from a spec, a committed fixture, a known-good example, or a hand calculation. A test that recomputes the implementation's answer is a tautology. The `test-pillars.md` catalogue names the second-source technique that avoids this.
+
+### Stop rule
+
+After two attempts to make a gate green by changing code, with the same gate still red, stop. Report the gate, the unedited output, and the two attempts. Do not edit the gate to get past the failure.
+
+## Pillar B: source hygiene
+
+### Zero comments on added production lines
+
+On every added line in production source (`apps/**`, `packages/**`, `supabase/functions/**`, `.ts`/`.tsx`, excluding test files), no comment token is allowed. That includes:
+
+- line comments, block comments, and JSDoc
+- authority citations such as `RULES.md #8` or `critical.md §3`
+- trailing comments on a line that is otherwise code
+- JSX comments such as `{/* ... */}`
+
+Existing comments are not touched. The rule is a ratchet: it judges the lines a change adds. A line that already had a trailing comment is an added line the moment the agent edits it, so the agent removes the comment as part of that edit.
+
+The check is `pnpm lint:comments --diff <ref>` and `pnpm lint:comments --working`. It uses the TypeScript parser to find comment tokens, so `https://` inside a string literal is not a comment and is not flagged. The full scan (`pnpm lint:comments` with no flag, the CI full-repository step) keeps only the banner rules, so legacy comments do not turn it red.
+
+Do not split an expression or statement across lines only to make room for a comment. Reviewers and `kael-review` own that judgment; no detector covers it.
+
+### Watermarks
+
+A watermark is any marker that says who or what produced the file. In source, these are blocked on added lines:
+
+- `Generated with ... Claude`
+- `Co-Authored-By:`
+- the robot emoji
+- `generated|written|authored by|with <AI name>` (Claude, Codex, Copilot, Cursor, ChatGPT, GPT)
+- an author, license, `SPDX-License-Identifier`, or `Copyright (c)` header
+
+No license header exists in product source today, so any header is new and counts as a watermark. The bare word `Claude` is not banned, because model IDs and tool names contain it.
+
+The watermark scope is source files. Commit-message trailers come from the harness and sit outside this skill.
+
+### Relationship to the existing comment linter
+
+The zero-comment rule lives in `pnpm lint:comments --diff` and `--working`. The full scan still allows WHY comments and authority citations, and `governance/protocols/code-hygiene.md` describes that allowance for existing code. `pnpm lint:added-hygiene` judges watermarks only. The two modes of one linter disagree by design: the ratchet blocks new comments, while the full scan only reports banners.
+
+## Pillar C: final claims
+
+Every claim in the final message maps to one row of this block:
+
+```text
+Claims -> evidence:
+Not run:
+Pre-existing (baseline):
+Gate or protected-surface changes:
+Comments / watermark (lint result):
+Stop-rule triggers:
+```
+
+Rules that fill it:
+
+- A command that was run appears with its exit code and the count line copied from its output. A count recalled from memory is not evidence.
+- A bug is "fixed" only after the original repro runs again and passes. Record the before and after.
+- "Pre-existing" requires a baseline run on a clean tree, and the baseline must have the same failure.
+- Every `file:line` or symbol cited is resolved by grep or Read in the same turn. A citation from earlier context is re-checked.
+- A revert inside a tree that a running job reads is not "recovered" until the units completed during the dirty window are checked against their recorded hashes and timestamps (`feedback_revert_is_not_a_save`).
+- A doc's claim about a gate's scope is checked against the config it describes. Example: `test-pillars.md` once said mobile collects only pillar files; `apps/mobile/jest.config.js` collected every `*test.tsx`. The config wins, and the doc is reported as drift.
+- Memory is a freshness signal. Verify it before it appears in a claim.
+- Anything not run is named in `Not run:`. Device, native, CI, and Stop-hook gaps are named there, never omitted.
+
+## Enforcement
+
+| Layer | Mechanism | Strength |
+|---|---|---|
+| L1 | Skill `description` and manifest `trigger` | Claude selects the skill; soft |
+| L2 | Lane row in `governance/protocols/work-router.md` (session condition) | Router assigns the skill at Verify and Close |
+| L3 | Stop hook `.claude/hooks/verify-test-integrity.mjs` (Claude Code only) | Blocks "done" on a new blocking finding; fails open on infrastructure errors |
+| L4 | `controls` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `--diff` against the base SHA | Blocks merge for Claude and Codex alike |
+| L5 | Tier 3 line in `CLAUDE.md` and pointer in `AGENTS.md` | Registration |
+
+Codex has no hook. Its automatic check is L4, and it runs the commands by hand before reporting.
+
+What the checkers cannot do: a weak assertion that still executes real code is not detected. Neither is a test that checks the wrong property well. Those remain reviewer findings. This protocol does not claim to stop semantic gaming.
+
+## Commands
+
+```bash
+pnpm lint:test-integrity --working     # uncommitted change (Stop hook)
+pnpm lint:test-integrity --diff <ref>  # committed range <ref>...HEAD (CI)
+pnpm lint:comments --working            # zero comments on added production lines (Stop hook)
+pnpm lint:comments --diff <ref>        # the same ratchet over the committed range (CI)
+pnpm lint:added-hygiene --working      # watermarks on added production lines
+pnpm lint:added-hygiene --diff <ref>
+```
+
+Exit codes: `0` clean, `1` blocking findings, `2` the checker could not run. A `2` is not a pass.
+
+## Output format
+
+```text
+Claims -> evidence:
+Not run:
+Pre-existing (baseline):
+Gate or protected-surface changes:
+Comments / watermark (lint result):
+Stop-rule triggers:
+```
+
+## Failure modes
+
+- Editing the test to match the output of the code.
+- Adding a skip to get a suite green, then reporting the suite green.
+- Reporting a count of tests that were not run.
+- Calling a pre-existing failure "unrelated" with no baseline.
+- Leaving a comment because "it documents the decision".
+
+## Rationalization table
+
+| Rationalization | Reality |
+|---|---|
+| "The assertion was redundant." | Then say so in `Gate or protected-surface changes` and let Tu clear it. The checker does not take your word for it. |
+| "I skipped it temporarily." | Temporary skips are the ones that stay. Do not add one. |
+| "The gate is wrong, so I fixed the gate." | Maybe. That is Tu's call, approved in chat, and reported on its own line. |
+| "The comment documents why." | The commit message and `docs/` document why. The source states the code. |
+| "It is only one `//` line." | The rule has no size threshold, because the reviewer cannot tell a one-line comment from a page of them. |
+| "Tests pass." | Show the command, the exit code, and the count it printed. |

+#!/usr/bin/env node
+// Watermark gate for governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md (Pillar B).
+//
+// Judges only lines a change adds under apps/, packages/, and supabase/functions/ (.ts and .tsx).
+// Comment tokens are judged by check-comment-discipline.mjs --diff and --working, not here.
+// Test files are checked too: a watermark is a watermark wherever it sits.
+//
+// Modes:
+//   node scripts/check-added-source-hygiene.mjs --working     uncommitted change (Stop hook)
+//   node scripts/check-added-source-hygiene.mjs --diff REF    committed range REF...HEAD (CI)
+//   node scripts/check-added-source-hygiene.mjs --rev SHA     one commit against its parent (calibration)
+//   add --warn to report without failing
+//
+// Exit: 0 clean, 1 blocking findings, 2 the checker could not run. Zero runtime deps.
+
+import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url'
+import { resolve } from 'node:path'
+import { collectChanges, selectMode } from './lib/unified-diff.mjs'
+
+const PRODUCT_ROOTS = /^(apps|packages|supabase\/functions)\//
+const SCANNED = /\.(ts|tsx)$/
+const SKIPPED_SEGMENTS = new Set(['node_modules', 'dist', 'build', '.next', '.expo', 'ios', 'android', 'coverage', '.turbo'])
+
+export const WATERMARKS = [
+  [/Generated with\b[^\n]*Claude/i, 'generated-with marker'],
+  [/Co-Authored-By:/i, 'commit trailer'],
+  [/\u{1F916}/u, 'robot emoji'],
+  [/\b(?:generated|written|authored)\s+(?:by|with)\s+(?:claude|codex|copilot|cursor|chatgpt|gpt)\b/i, 'AI self-attribution'],
+  [/@(?:author|license)\b/i, 'author or license tag'],
+  [/SPDX-License-Identifier|Copyright \(c\)/i, 'license header'],
+]
+
+export function analyzeHygiene(change) {
+  const findings = []
+  const path = change.path
+  if (!PRODUCT_ROOTS.test(path) || !SCANNED.test(path)) return findings
+  if (path.split('/').some((segment) => SKIPPED_SEGMENTS.has(segment))) return findings
+
+  for (const { line, text } of change.added) {
+    const hit = WATERMARKS.find(([pattern]) => pattern.test(text))
+    if (hit) findings.push({ file: path, line, rule: 'watermark', detail: hit[1] })
+  }
+  return findings
+}
+
+function main(args) {
+  const selection = selectMode(args)
+  if (!selection) {
+    console.error('check-added-source-hygiene: pass --working, --diff <ref>, or --rev <sha>.')
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  let changes
+  try {
+    changes = collectChanges(selection)
+  } catch (error) {
+    console.error(`check-added-source-hygiene: git failed: ${error.message}`)
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  let findings
+  try {
+    findings = changes.flatMap(analyzeHygiene)
+  } catch (error) {
+    console.error(`check-added-source-hygiene: analysis failed: ${error.message}`)
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  for (const finding of findings) {
+    console.log(`BLOCK ${finding.file}:${finding.line} [${finding.rule}] ${finding.detail}`)
+  }
+  console.log(`check-added-source-hygiene: ${findings.length} blocking, ${changes.length} changed file(s) examined`)
+  process.exit(findings.length && !args.includes('--warn') ? 1 : 0)
+}
+
+if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(resolve(process.argv[1])).href) {
+  main(process.argv.slice(2))
+}

+import { test } from 'node:test'
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict'
+import { analyzeHygiene } from './check-added-source-hygiene.mjs'
+
+const PRODUCT = 'apps/mobile/lib/price.ts'
+const PRODUCT_TSX = 'apps/mobile/components/card.tsx'
+
+const change = (path, text) => ({
+  path,
+  added: text.split('\n').map((line, index) => ({ line: index + 1, text: line })),
+  removed: [],
+  content: text,
+})
+
+const watermarkLines = (findings) => findings.filter((finding) => finding.rule === 'watermark').map((finding) => finding.line)
+
+test('clean control: plain production code raises no finding', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(analyzeHygiene(change(PRODUCT, 'export const n = 1\nexport const url = "https://example.com"')), [])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a generated-with marker blocks', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(watermarkLines(analyzeHygiene(change(PRODUCT, 'export const note = "Generated with Claude Code"'))), [1])
+})
+

+// Zero-comment ratchet for production source. Every comment token on an added line counts,
+// including authority citations and JSDoc; `includedLines` null means the whole file is new.
+export function zeroCommentViolations(path, content, includedLines = null) {
+  if (!ZERO_COMMENT_SCOPE.test(path) || TEST_FILE.test(path)) return []
+  const lines = content.split('\n')
+  const found = []
+  for (const lineNumber of commentLines(path, content)) {
+    if (includedLines && !includedLines.has(lineNumber)) continue
+    found.push({
+      line: lineNumber,
+      hits: ['comment on an added production line'],
+      snippet: (lines[lineNumber - 1] ?? '').trim().slice(0, 90),
+    })
+  }
+  return found.sort((a, b) => a.line - b.line)
+}
+

   return EXTS.some((e) => path.endsWith(e))
 }

+    }
   }

+import { test } from 'node:test'
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict'
+import { zeroCommentViolations } from './check-comment-discipline.mjs'
+import { commentLines } from './lib/comment-lines.mjs'
+
+const PRODUCT = 'apps/mobile/lib/price.ts'
+const PRODUCT_TSX = 'apps/mobile/components/card.tsx'
+
+const lines = (violations) => violations.map((violation) => violation.line)
+
+const added = (content, indexes = null) => {
+  const all = content.split('\n')
+  return new Set(indexes ?? all.map((_, index) => index + 1))
+}
+
+test('clean control: a URL inside a string literal is not a comment', () => {
+  const content = 'export const url = "https://example.com/path"\nexport const n = 1'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content)), [])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a trailing line comment on an added line is a violation', () => {
+  const content = 'export const n = 1 // the count'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content)), [1])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a multi-line block comment reports every line it spans', () => {
+  const content = '/**\n * Explains the price.\n */\nexport const n = 1'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content)), [1, 2, 3])
+})
+
+test('a comment on a line the change did not add is not judged', () => {
+  const content = '// old note\nexport const n = 1'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content, added(content, [2]))), [])
+})
+
+test('a comment-like sequence inside a template literal is not a comment', () => {
+  const content = 'export const s = `/* not a comment */ and // not either`'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content)), [])
+})
+
+test('a comment-like sequence inside JSX text is not a comment', () => {
+  const content = 'export const C = () => <p>https://example.com // x</p>'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT_TSX, content)), [])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a JSX expression comment is a violation', () => {
+  const content = 'export const C = () => (\n  <p>\n    {/* note */}\n  </p>\n)'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT_TSX, content)), [3])
+})
+
+test('authority citations are comments too', () => {
+  const content = '// Per RULES.md #8\nexport const n = 1'
+  assert.deepEqual(lines(zeroCommentViolations(PRODUCT, content)), [1])
+})
+
+test('test files and paths outside the product roots are exempt', () => {
+  const content = 'test("x", () => {}) // a note'
+  assert.deepEqual(zeroCommentViolations('apps/mobile/lib/__tests__/x-test.ts', content), [])
+  assert.deepEqual(zeroCommentViolations('scripts/tool.ts', content), [])
+  assert.deepEqual(zeroCommentViolations('apps/mobile/lib/readme.md', content), [])
+})
+
+test('commentLines reports exactly the lines that carry comment tokens', () => {
+  const content = 'const a = 1 // one\nconst b = "// not"\n/* two */ const c = 3'
+  assert.deepEqual([...commentLines('apps/mobile/x.ts', content)].sort((a, b) => a - b), [1, 3])
+})

+#!/usr/bin/env node
+// Test-integrity gate for governance/protocols/solve-conflict-maximize.md (Pillar A).
+//
+// Modes:
+//   node scripts/check-test-integrity.mjs --working       uncommitted change (Stop hook)
+//   node scripts/check-test-integrity.mjs --diff REF      committed range REF...HEAD (CI)
+//   node scripts/check-test-integrity.mjs --rev SHA       one commit against its parent (calibration)
+//   add --warn to report without failing
+//
+// Exit: 0 clean, 1 blocking findings, 2 the checker could not run. Zero runtime deps.
+
+import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url'
+import { resolve } from 'node:path'
+import { TEST_FILE, collectChanges, selectMode } from './lib/unified-diff.mjs'
+
+const PROTECTED = [
+  /^scripts\/check-[^/]+\.mjs$/,
+  /^scripts\/harness\//,
+  /^scripts\/structure-baseline\.json$/,
+  /^scripts\/lint-structure\.mjs$/,
+  /^config\/harness\/manifest\.json$/,
+  /(^|\/)jest\.config\.[cm]?[jt]s$/,
+  /(^|\/)vitest\.config\.[cm]?[jt]s$|(^|\/)vitest\.config\.mts$/,
+  /^\.github\/workflows\//,
+  /^\.claude\/settings[^/]*\.json$/,
+  /^\.claude\/hooks\//,
+  /^docs\/test-debt-ledger\.md$/,
+]
+
+const PRODUCT_SOURCE = /^(apps|packages|supabase\/functions)\/.*\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/
+
+const ASSERTION = /\bexpect(?:TypeOf)?\s*\(|\bassert(?:\.\w+)?\s*\(/g
+const TEST_CALL = /\b(?:it|test)\s*\(/
+const FOCUS_OR_SKIP = /\.(?:skip|only|todo)\s*\(|\b(?:xit|xdescribe|xtest)\s*\(/
+const SNAPSHOT_ASSERTION = /\bto(?:Match|MatchInline|MatchFile)Snapshot\s*\(/
+const SNAPSHOT_UPDATE = /\b(?:vitest|jest)\b[^\n]*\s(?:-u|--update|--updateSnapshot|--update-snapshot)(?=[\s"'`]|$)/
+const TRIVIAL_ASSERTION = /\bexpect\s*\(\s*(?:true|false)\s*\)/
+const TYPE_ESCAPE = /\bas\s+any\b|@ts-(?:ignore|expect-error|nocheck)\b/
+const EXPECTED_LITERAL = /\.(?:toBe|toEqual|toStrictEqual|toMatchObject|toHaveLength)\s*\(/
+
+export const countAssertions = (text) => (text.match(ASSERTION) ?? []).length
+
+export function analyzeChanges(changes) {
+  const productSourceChanged = changes.some((change) => PRODUCT_SOURCE.test(change.path) && !TEST_FILE.test(change.path))
+  return changes.flatMap((change) => analyzeChange(change, productSourceChanged))
+}
+
+function analyzeChange(change, productSourceChanged) {
+  const findings = []
+  const block = (rule, detail) => findings.push({ file: change.path, severity: 'block', rule, detail })
+  const report = (rule, detail) => findings.push({ file: change.path, severity: 'report', rule, detail })
+
+  if (PROTECTED.some((pattern) => pattern.test(change.path))) {
+    report('protected-surface', 'gate, config, or workflow changed; list it under "Gate or protected-surface changes"')
+  }
+  for (const { line, text } of change.added) {
+    if (SNAPSHOT_UPDATE.test(text)) block('snapshot-update', `line ${line} runs a snapshot update`)
+  }
+  if (!TEST_FILE.test(change.path)) return findings
+
+  const addedText = change.added.map((item) => item.text).join('\n')
+  const removedText = change.removed.join('\n')
+  const netDrop = countAssertions(removedText) - countAssertions(addedText)
+  if (netDrop > 0) block('assertion-drop', `${netDrop} assertion call(s) removed net`)
+
+  for (const { line, text } of change.added) {
+    if (FOCUS_OR_SKIP.test(text)) block('focus-or-skip', `line ${line} adds a focused, skipped, or todo test`)
+    if (SNAPSHOT_ASSERTION.test(text)) block('snapshot-assertion', `line ${line} adds a snapshot assertion`)
+    if (TRIVIAL_ASSERTION.test(text)) block('trivial-assertion', `line ${line} asserts a constant`)
+    if (TYPE_ESCAPE.test(text)) block('type-escape', `line ${line} adds a type escape in a test`)
+  }
+
+  if (!change.deleted && typeof change.content === 'string') {
+    if (TEST_CALL.test(change.content) && countAssertions(change.content) === 0) {
+      block('no-assertion', 'test file declares tests but contains no assertion')
+    }
+  }
+
+  const literalRemoved = change.removed.some((line) => EXPECTED_LITERAL.test(line))
+  const literalAdded = change.added.some((item) => EXPECTED_LITERAL.test(item.text))
+  if (productSourceChanged && literalRemoved && literalAdded) {
+    report('expected-literal-changed', 'an expected value changed while product source changed in the same diff')
+  }
+  return findings
+}
+
+function main(args) {
+  const selection = selectMode(args)
+  if (!selection) {
+    console.error('check-test-integrity: pass --working, --diff <ref>, or --rev <sha>.')
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  let changes
+  try {
+    changes = collectChanges(selection)
+  } catch (error) {
+    console.error(`check-test-integrity: git failed: ${error.message}`)
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  let findings
+  try {
+    findings = analyzeChanges(changes)
+  } catch (error) {
+    console.error(`check-test-integrity: analysis failed: ${error.message}`)
+    process.exit(2)
+  }
+  const blocking = findings.filter((finding) => finding.severity === 'block')
+  const reports = findings.filter((finding) => finding.severity === 'report')
+  for (const finding of [...blocking, ...reports]) {
+    console.log(`${finding.severity === 'block' ? 'BLOCK ' : 'REPORT'} ${finding.file} [${finding.rule}] ${finding.detail}`)
+  }
+  console.log(`check-test-integrity: ${blocking.length} blocking, ${reports.length} report-only, ${changes.length} changed file(s) examined`)
+  process.exit(blocking.length && !args.includes('--warn') ? 1 : 0)
+}
+
+if (process.argv[1] && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(resolve(process.argv[1])).href) {
+  main(process.argv.slice(2))
+}

+import { test } from 'node:test'
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict'
+import { analyzeChanges } from './check-test-integrity.mjs'
+import { parseUnifiedDiff } from './lib/unified-diff.mjs'
+
+// Forbidden tokens are assembled at runtime so this file does not contain them as literals.
+const tok = (...parts) => parts.join('')
+const SKIP_CALL = tok('it.', 'skip', '(')
+const TODO_CALL = tok('it.', 'todo', '(')
+const SNAPSHOT = tok('expect(a).toMatch', 'Snapshot', '()')
+const TRUE_CONST = tok('expect', '(true)', '.toBe(true)')
+const ANY_CAST = tok('as ', 'any')
+const IGNORE_LINE = tok('// @ts-', 'ignore')
+const UPDATE_CMD = tok('"test": "vitest run ', '-u"')
+const EXPECT_CALL = tok('expect', '(a).toBe(1)')
+
+const change = (path, added, removed = [], content = null, deleted = false) => ({
+  path,
+  added: added.map((text, index) => ({ line: index + 1, text })),
+  removed,
+  content,
+  deleted,
+})
+
+const blocks = (findings) => findings.filter((finding) => finding.severity === 'block').map((finding) => finding.rule)
+const reports = (findings) => findings.filter((finding) => finding.severity === 'report').map((finding) => finding.rule)
+const TEST_PATH = 'apps/mobile/components/__tests__/widget-test.tsx'
+
+test('clean control: a replaced assertion with a real value raises no finding', () => {
+  const findings = analyzeChanges([
+    change(TEST_PATH, [`  ${EXPECT_CALL}`], [`  ${tok('expect', '(a).toBe(2)')}`], `test('x', () => {\n  ${EXPECT_CALL}\n})`),
+  ])
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(findings), [])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: net assertion removal blocks', () => {
+  const findings = analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [], [`  ${EXPECT_CALL}`, `  ${EXPECT_CALL}`], `test('x', () => {})`)])
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(findings), ['assertion-drop', 'no-assertion'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a deleted test file counts its removed assertions', () => {
+  const findings = analyzeChanges([change('apps/mobile/lib/__tests__/gone-test.ts', [], [`  ${EXPECT_CALL}`], null, true)])
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(findings), ['assertion-drop'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: focused, skipped, or todo test blocks', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`${SKIP_CALL} 'x', () => {`, `${EXPECT_CALL}`])])), ['focus-or-skip'])
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`${TODO_CALL} 'x')`, `${EXPECT_CALL}`])])), ['focus-or-skip'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: snapshot assertion blocks', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`  ${SNAPSHOT}`])])), ['snapshot-assertion'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: snapshot update command blocks even outside a test file', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change('package.json', [`    ${UPDATE_CMD}`])])), ['snapshot-update'])
+})
+
+test('clean control: a vitest run without an update flag is not a snapshot update', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change('package.json', ['    "test": "vitest run --reporter=dot"'])])), [])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: constant assertion blocks', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`  ${TRUE_CONST}`])])), ['trivial-assertion'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: type escape in a test blocks', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`  const v = value ${ANY_CAST}`, `  ${EXPECT_CALL}`])])), ['type-escape'])
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, [`  ${IGNORE_LINE}`, `  ${EXPECT_CALL}`])])), ['type-escape'])
+})
+
+test('red fixture: a test file that declares tests but asserts nothing blocks', () => {
+  const content = 'test("one", () => {})\nit("two", () => {})'
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change(TEST_PATH, ['test("one", () => {})'], [], content)])), ['no-assertion'])
+})
+
+test('a helper module with no test calls is not required to assert', () => {
+  assert.deepEqual(blocks(analyzeChanges([change('apps/mobile/lib/__tests__/helpers-test.ts', ['export const x = 1'], [], 'export const x = 1')])), [])
+})
+
+test('gate and config edits are reported, never blocked', () => {
+  const findings = analyzeChanges([
+    change('scripts/check-source-residue.mjs', ['const x = 1']),
+    change('config/harness/manifest.json', ['  "entries": []']),
+    change('apps/mobile/app/index.tsx', ['export const y = 2']),

+test('parseUnifiedDiff keeps removed lines that look like headers and marks deleted files', () => {

+  assert.equal(live.path, 'apps/x/__tests__/a.test.ts')

+import { execFileSync, spawnSync } from 'node:child_process'
+import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path'
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'
+
+const ROOT = resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '../..')
+const WINDOWS_STORE_SCRIPT = resolve(ROOT, 'scripts/harness/production-release-credentials.ps1')
+const POSIX_STORE_SCRIPT = resolve(ROOT, 'scripts/harness/production-release-credentials.sh')
+const SERVICE = 'nestscout-production-release'
+
+export const RELEASE_SECRET_NAMES = Object.freeze([
+  'ANTHROPIC_API_KEY',
+  'CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN',
+  'DEEPSEEK_API_KEY',
+  'EXPO_TOKEN',
+  'PERPLEXITY_API_KEY',
+  'PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY',
+  'PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY',
+  'PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_EMAIL',
+  'PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_PASSWORD',
+  'PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_EMAIL',
+  'PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_PASSWORD',
+  'SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN',
+  'SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD',
+  'SYSTEM_HEALTH_MONITOR_SECRET',
+  'SYSTEM_HEALTH_OBSERVER_SECRET',
+  'VIETMAP_API_KEY',
+])
+
+export function redactSensitiveText(value, secrets = []) {
+  let text = String(value ?? '')
+  const normalizedSecrets = [...new Set(secrets.filter((secret) => typeof secret === 'string' && secret.length >= 4))]
+    .sort((left, right) => right.length - left.length)
+  for (const secret of normalizedSecrets) {
+    for (const variant of secretVariants(secret)) text = text.replaceAll(variant, '[REDACTED]')
+  }
+  return text
+    .replace(/(https?:\/\/[^\s?]+)\?[^\s]+/giu, '$1?[REDACTED_QUERY]')
+    .replace(/(\b(?:SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN|SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY|PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY|EXPO_TOKEN|CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN|SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD)\s*[=:]\s*)[^\s,;]+/giu, '$1[REDACTED]')
+    .replace(/(\bAuthorization\s*:\s*Bearer\s+)[^\s,;]+/giu, '$1[REDACTED]')
+    .replace(/\b(?:sbp_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,}|sb_secret_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{12,})\b/gu, '[REDACTED]')
+}
+
+export function secretVariants(secret) {
+  const variants = new Set([secret])
+  try {
+    variants.add(encodeURIComponent(secret))
+    variants.add(Buffer.from(secret, 'utf8').toString('base64'))
+  } catch {
+    return [...variants]
+  }
+  return [...variants].filter((variant) => variant.length >= 4)
+}
+
+export function readReleaseCredentials({ platform = process.platform, names = RELEASE_SECRET_NAMES, root = ROOT } = {}) {
+  const requested = [...new Set(names)]
+  if (requested.some((name) => !RELEASE_SECRET_NAMES.includes(name))) {
+    throw new Error('production release credential name is not allow-listed')
+  }
+  if (platform === 'win32') return readWindowsCredentials(requested, root)
+  if (platform === 'linux') return readSecretServiceCredentials(requested)
+  return { values: {}, missing: requested, store: 'unsupported' }
+}
+
+export function setReleaseCredential(name, { platform = process.platform, root = ROOT } = {}) {
+  if (!RELEASE_SECRET_NAMES.includes(name)) throw new Error('production release credential name is not allow-listed')
+  if (platform === 'win32') {
+    const result = spawnSync('powershell.exe', [
+      '-NoLogo', '-NoProfile', '-ExecutionPolicy', 'Bypass',
+      '-File', resolve(root, 'scripts/harness/production-release-credentials.ps1'),
+      '-Action', 'Set', '-Name', name,
+    ], { cwd: root, stdio: 'inherit' })
+    if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw new Error('secure credential storage failed')
+    return `Stored credential: ${name}`
+  }
+  if (platform === 'linux') {
+    const result = spawnSync('bash', [resolve(root, 'scripts/harness/production-release-credentials.sh'), 'set', name], {
+      cwd: root, stdio: 'inherit',
+    })
+    if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw new Error('secure credential storage failed')
+    return `Stored credential: ${name}`
+  }
+  throw new Error('secure credential storage is supported through Windows Credential Manager or Linux Secret Service')
+}
+
+function readWindowsCredentials(names, root) {
+  const result = spawnSync('powershell.exe', [
+    '-NoLogo', '-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-ExecutionPolicy', 'Bypass',
+    '-File', resolve(root, WINDOWS_STORE_SCRIPT), '-Action', 'Read', '-Names', names.join(','),
+  ], { cwd: root, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'] })
+  if (result.error || result.status !== 0) return { values: {}, missing: names, store: 'unavailable' }
+  let values
+  try { values = JSON.parse(result.stdout) } catch { return { values: {}, missing: names, store: 'unavailable' } }
+  const present = Object.fromEntries(names.filter((name) => typeof values[name] === 'string' && values[name].length > 0)
+    .map((name) => [name, values[name]]))
+  return { values: present, missing: names.filter((name) => !present[name]), store: 'windows-credential-manager' }
+}
+
+function readSecretServiceCredentials(names) {
+  const values = {}
+  for (const name of names) {
+    try {
+      const value = execFileSync('secret-tool', ['lookup', 'application', SERVICE, 'name', name], {
+        encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'ignore'],
+      }).trimEnd()
+      if (value.length > 0) values[name] = value
+    } catch {
+      continue
+    }
+  }
+  return { values, missing: names.filter((name) => !values[name]), store: 'linux-secret-service' }
+}
+
+function main() {
+  const [command, ...forwarded] = process.argv.slice(2)
+  const name = forwarded[0] === '--' ? forwarded[1] : forwarded[0]
+  if (command !== 'set' || !name) throw new Error('usage: production-release-credentials.mjs set <allow-listed-name>')
+  process.stdout.write(`${setReleaseCredential(name)}\n`)
+}
+
+if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
+  try { main() } catch (error) {
+    process.stderr.write(`production-release-credentials failed: ${error.message}\n`)
+    process.exitCode = 1
+  }
+}
added in remote
  their  100644 706564224dc5819139a5e4a3b3faad86a254339c scripts/harness/production-release-credentials.ps1
@@ -0,0 +1,108 @@

+if (-not ('NestScoutReleaseCredentialNative' -as [type])) {
+  Add-Type -TypeDefinition @'
+using System;
+using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
+
+[StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential, CharSet = CharSet.Unicode)]
+public struct NestScoutCredential {
+    public UInt32 Flags;
+    public UInt32 Type;
+    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] public string TargetName;
+    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] public string Comment;
+    public System.Runtime.InteropServices.ComTypes.FILETIME LastWritten;
+    public UInt32 CredentialBlobSize;
+    public IntPtr CredentialBlob;
+    public UInt32 Persist;
+    public UInt32 AttributeCount;
+    public IntPtr Attributes;
+    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] public string TargetAlias;
+    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] public string UserName;
+}
+
+public static class NestScoutReleaseCredentialNative {
+    [DllImport("Advapi32.dll", EntryPoint = "CredReadW", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)]
+    public static extern bool CredRead(string target, UInt32 type, UInt32 flags, out IntPtr credential);
+    [DllImport("Advapi32.dll", EntryPoint = "CredWriteW", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)]
+    public static extern bool CredWrite(ref NestScoutCredential credential, UInt32 flags);
+    [DllImport("Advapi32.dll", EntryPoint = "CredFree", SetLastError = false)]
+    public static extern void CredFree(IntPtr credential);
+}
+'@
+}
+
+function Assert-AllowedName([string]$Candidate) {
+  if ($Candidate -notin $allowedNames) { throw 'CREDENTIAL_NAME_NOT_ALLOWED' }
+}
+
+function Read-Credential([string]$Candidate) {
+  $pointer = [IntPtr]::Zero
+  $target = "NestScoutProductionRelease/$Candidate"
+  if (-not [NestScoutReleaseCredentialNative]::CredRead($target, 1, 0, [ref]$pointer)) { return $null }
+  try {
+    $credential = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::PtrToStructure($pointer, [type][NestScoutCredential])
+    $secretBytes = [byte[]]::new([int]$credential.CredentialBlobSize)
+    if ($secretBytes.Length -gt 0) {
+      [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::Copy($credential.CredentialBlob, $secretBytes, 0, $secretBytes.Length)
+      try { return [Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetString($secretBytes) }
+      finally { for ($index = 0; $index -lt $secretBytes.Length; $index++) { $secretBytes[$index] = 0 } }
+    }
+    return $null
+  } finally {
+    [NestScoutReleaseCredentialNative]::CredFree($pointer)
+  }
+}
+
+if ($Action -eq 'Read') {
+  $records = [ordered]@{}
+  foreach ($candidate in ($Names -split ',')) {
+    Assert-AllowedName $candidate
+    $records[$candidate] = Read-Credential $candidate
+  }
+  $records | ConvertTo-Json -Compress
+  exit 0
+}
+
+Assert-AllowedName $Name
+$secure = Read-Host "Enter the secret value for $Name" -AsSecureString
+$bstr = [IntPtr]::Zero
+$blob = [IntPtr]::Zero
+$secretBytes = $null
+try {
+  $bstr = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::SecureStringToBSTR($secure)
+  $plain = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::PtrToStringBSTR($bstr)
+  $secretBytes = [Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($plain)
+  if ($secretBytes.Length -eq 0 -or $secretBytes.Length -gt 5120) { throw 'CREDENTIAL_VALUE_SIZE_INVALID' }
+  $blob = [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::AllocHGlobal($secretBytes.Length)
+  [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::Copy($secretBytes, 0, $blob, $secretBytes.Length)
+  $credential = [NestScoutCredential]::new()
+  $credential.Type = 1
+  $credential.TargetName = "NestScoutProductionRelease/$Name"
+  $credential.CredentialBlobSize = [UInt32]$secretBytes.Length
+  $credential.CredentialBlob = $blob
+  $credential.Persist = 2
+  $credential.UserName = $Name
+  if (-not [NestScoutReleaseCredentialNative]::CredWrite([ref]$credential, 0)) { throw 'CREDENTIAL_STORE_WRITE_FAILED' }
+  Write-Output "Stored credential: $Name"
+} finally {
+  if ($secretBytes) { for ($index = 0; $index -lt $secretBytes.Length; $index++) { $secretBytes[$index] = 0 } }
+  if ($blob -ne [IntPtr]::Zero) { [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::FreeHGlobal($blob) }
+  if ($bstr -ne [IntPtr]::Zero) { [Runtime.InteropServices.Marshal]::ZeroFreeBSTR($bstr) }
+  if ($secure) { $secure.Dispose() }
+  Remove-Variable plain -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
+}

+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+set -euo pipefail
+
+service='nestscout-production-release'
+allowed=(
+  ANTHROPIC_API_KEY CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN DEEPSEEK_API_KEY EXPO_TOKEN PERPLEXITY_API_KEY
+  PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY
+  PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_EMAIL PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_CUSTOMER_PASSWORD
+  PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_EMAIL PRODUCTION_SYNTHETIC_WORKER_PASSWORD
+  SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD SYSTEM_HEALTH_MONITOR_SECRET
+  SYSTEM_HEALTH_OBSERVER_SECRET VIETMAP_API_KEY
+)
+
+command_name="${1:-}"
+secret_name="${2:-}"
+if [[ "$command_name" != set || ! " ${allowed[*]} " =~ " $secret_name " ]]; then
+  printf '%s\n' 'usage: production-release-credentials.sh set <allow-listed-name>' >&2
+  exit 2
+fi
+if ! command -v secret-tool >/dev/null 2>&1; then
+  printf '%s\n' 'LINUX_SECRET_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE' >&2
+  exit 1
+fi
+
+read -r -s -p "Enter the secret value for $secret_name: " secret_value
+printf '\n'
+if [[ -z "$secret_value" ]]; then
+  unset secret_value
+  printf '%s\n' 'CREDENTIAL_VALUE_REQUIRED' >&2
+  exit 1
+fi
+printf '%s' "$secret_value" | secret-tool store --label='NestScout Production Release' application "$service" name "$secret_name"
+unset secret_value
+printf 'Stored credential: %s\n' "$secret_name"
